=== idx: 2 ===
Gold supporting: the ablation of a muscle - specific sarcoplasmic reticulum ca2 + atpase ( serca2a ) inhibitor, phospholamban, rescued the spectrum of phenotypes that resemble human heart failure.
LLM supporting:  the main regulatory molecule of serca2a function in the cardiac muscle is phospholamban. phospholamban is a small, 16. 5 kda protein that is natively bound to the sarcoplasmic reticulum calcium atpase ( serca2a ), modulating its activity. under resting conditions, phospholamban inhibits serca2a, thereby controlling the rate of calcium uptake into the sarcoplasmic reticulum. this inhibition is crucial for maintaining the balance of intracellular calcium, which is essential for proper cardiac muscle function. when phospholamban is phosphorylated, it releases from serca2a, leading to increased serca2a activity and enhanced calcium reuptake, which can be important during periods of increased cellular demand, such as muscle contraction. the interaction between phospholamban and serca2a is a key regulatory point in cardiac excitation - contraction coupling, and disturbances in this interaction have been implicated in various cardiac pathologies, including dilated cardiomyopathy.
